❯ reorganize this repository to a standard python package setup as follows:     
  1. make it pip installable                                                    
  2. store source code in a directory called src                                
  3. leave the main training/tuning script in a file called tune.py in the root 
  directory                                                                     
  4. put the ipynb notebook in a directory called notebooks                     
  5. put all tests in a directory called tests - use pytest to rigorously test  
  against the setups outlined in the project outline (30x30 gridworld size etc) 
  6. Ensure each function has a rigorous test in the test directory - check     
  carefully for 0 div errors (log of negatives etc).                            
  7. Install numba so that we can compile slow code in the training loop        
  8. Create a robust perf benchmark to ensure that the code required for the    
  project (as laid out in our proposal and in the instructions) runs in a time  
  window no more than 10min or explain why we cannot meet this limitation. Also 
  check for memory limitations - consider using optimizations to reduce memory  
  impact of large state spaces or long rollouts                                 
  9. Keep the structure of the enhancements being in separate directories.      
  10. ensure proper type annotation and type checking. We should pass a linting 
  check.                                                                        
  11. Ensure the readme documentation outlines how to install, how the problem  
  is setup, and what/where the enhancements are                                 
  12. for now leave the bregman divergences enhancements blank, I will provide  
  code to guide that later. For now, update the training for SPMD & TOMC to     
  allow different bregman divergence functions to be passed as arguments        
  (dependency injection style- so shared func signatures / maybe a using an     
  abstract base class as prototype?). You can create the abstract base class    
  now if you think that is a good approach.                                     
  13. If you are unsure of a good way to organize shared code across            
  enhancements, just ask before proceeding

     size should the code target? → Configurable, 10x10 default (Recommended)

     (10x10 keeps the existing hand-placed layout either way.) → Scale with area
     (Recommended)
     · How should shared code be organized under src/? (Please check the 
     preview.) → core + envs + enhancements

     so tune.py and the benchmark can run it the same way as TOMC? The proposal
     compares it as an enhancement, but right now it only runs standalone on a 
     fixed policy. → Yes, pluggable (Recommended)

  ⎿  Created src/ie692_rl/__init__.py +10
       1 +"""IE 692 reinforcement-learning project.                      
       2 +                                                               
       3 +Subpackages:                                                   
       4 +    envs          GridWorld (any size), simplified Blackjack, M
         +5 retail inventory                                             
       5 +    core          baseline TOMC estimator, SPMD loop, Bregman d
         +ivergence interface (KL), exact scoring                        
       6 +    enhancements  bregman_spmd, true_online_td, entropy_reg_spm
         +d

         +e root ``tune.py``                                             
       8 +"""                                                            
       9 +                                                               
      10 +__version__ = "0.1.0"                                          
  ⎿  Created src/ie692_rl/_jit.py +16
       1 +"""Typed wrapper around numba.njit.                            
       2 +                                                               
       3 +numba ships no type stubs, so a bare ``@numba.njit`` erases the
         + decorated function's signature for                            
       4 +mypy. ``njit`` returns the function with its annotations intact
         + while still compiling it.                                     
       5 +Kernels are cached on disk (``cache=True``) so worker processes
         + do not recompile them.                                        
       6 +"""                                                            
       7 +                                                               
       8 +from collections.abc import Callable                           
       9 +from typing import Any, cast                                   
      10 +                                                               
      11 +import numba                                                   
      12 +                                                               
      13 +                                                               
      14 +def njit[F: Callable[..., Any]](function: F) -> F:             
      15 +    """Compile ``function`` in nopython mode with an on-disk ca
         +che, keeping its static type."""                               
      16 +    return cast(F, numba.njit(cache=True)(function))           
  ⎿  Created src/ie692_rl/types.py +8
      1 +"""Array aliases shared across the package."""                  
      2 +                                                                
      3 +import numpy as np                                              
      4 +import numpy.typing as npt                                      
      5 +                                                                
      6 +FloatArray = npt.NDArray[np.float64]                            
      7 +IntArray = npt.NDArray[np.int64]                                
      8 +BoolArray = npt.NDArray[np.bool_]                               

⏺ Write(src/ie692_rl/envs/base.py)
  ⎿  Wrote 142 lines to src/ie692_rl/envs/base.py
       1 """Common interface for the project's finite MDPs.
       2 
       3 Each environment is an online simulator: algorithms only ever se
         e sampled
       4 ``(next_state, cost, done)`` transitions. To make sampling fast,
          every environment exposes its rules
       5 as a numba ``Kernel``:
       6 
       7     step(params, state, action, rng) -> (next_state, cost, done)
       8     reset(params, rng) -> start state of a new stream / episode
     … +134 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

        1 -"""10x10 GridWorld problem.                                   
        1 +"""Square GridWorld from Lecture 1, enlarged as the project ha
          +ndout asks.                                                   
        2 
        3 -Actions: 0=up, 1=down, 2=left, 3=right.                       
        4 -Costs are charged at the current state. Goal/hazard states res
          -et to start                                                   
        5 -with probability 0.8; otherwise movement occurs normally.     
        3 +Actions: 0=up, 1=down, 2=left, 3=right. The intended move happ
          +ens with probability ``p``;                                   
        4 +otherwise one of the other three directions is taken uniformly
          +. Walls clip movement.                                        
        5 +The cost is charged at the current state: 0 at a goal, 1 at a 
          +hazard, 0.2 elsewhere. From a goal or                         
        6 +hazard the agent is sent back to the start with probability ``
          +1 - q``; otherwise it moves normally.                         
        7 +The task never terminates.                                    
        8 +                                                              
        9 +``size=10`` is the handout's layout (10 hazards, 3 goals, star
          +t in the top-left corner). Other sizes                        
       10 +keep the start in the top-left corner and goals in the other t
          +hree corners, and place                                       
       11 +``round(hazard_density * size**2)`` hazards (10% by default, s
          +o 90 on a 30x30 grid) uniformly at                            
       12 +random with a fixed ``layout_seed``, never on the start or a g
          +oal.                                                          
       13  """
       14 
       15 +from typing import Any                                        
       16 +                                                              
       17  import numpy as np
       18 
       10 -class GridWorld:                                              
       11 -    def __init__(self, p=0.8, q=0.2, gamma=0.9):              
       12 -        self.rows = 10

         +nlineTD``).

       1 +"""Tabular stochastic policies stored as pi[state, action]."""

         +robabilities summing to 1")

         +g: np.random.Generator) -> int:                                
      29 +    """Draw an action from one policy row with a single uniform
         + number.                                                       
      30 +                                                               
      31 +    Actions with probability 0 are never returned. If rounding 
         +leaves the row's total slightly                                
      32 +    below the uniform draw, the last action with positive proba
         +bility is used.                                                
      33 +    """                                                        
      34 +    u = rng.random()                                           
      35 +    last = 0                                                   
      36 +    for a in range(cdf_row.shape[0]):                          
      37 +        if prob_row[a] > 0.0:                                  
      38 +            if u < cdf_row[a]:                                 
      39 +                return a                                       
      40 +            last = a

        1 -""" Baseline components integration:                          
        1 +"""Baseline policy evaluation: Truncated On-Policy Monte Carlo
          + (TOMC) with dynamic exploration time.                        
        2 
        3 -1. Implement a Truncated On-Policy Monte Carlo (TOMC) with dyn
          -amic exploration time for                                     
        4 -Policy Evaluation. More details about tuning given in the Deta
          -ils section;                                                  
        5 -2. Implement a tuned Stochastic Policy Mirror Descent (SPMD) f
          -or Policy Optimization;                                       
        3 +One estimate follows the current policy in a single on-policy 
          +stream (a new Blackjack hand is dealt                         
        4 +after each finished one) until                                
        5 
        7 -Policies and Q estimates are [state, action]; costs are minimi
          -zed.                                                          
        6 +  1. every *required* pair, pi(a|s) >= pi_min (default (1 - ga
          +mma) / |A|), has been visited, and                            
        7 +  2. the discount factor of the tail after the most recent fir
          +st visit, gamma^(n - t_last), is at                           
        8 +     most ``bias * (1 - gamma)``, so truncating the stream cha
          +nges no first-visit return by more                            
        9 +     than ``bias`` times the cost bound.                      
       10 +                                                              
       11 +Q(s, a) is the discounted return after the first visit to (s, 
          +a) (it stops at the end of a                                  
       12 +Blackjack hand). Pairs that are not required get the pessimist
          +ic value ``cost_bound / (1 - gamma)``.                        
       13 +                                                              
       14 +Memory: the reference implementation stored the whole stream, 
          +which for long streams (30x30                                 
       15 +GridWorld needs tens of millions of steps) costs gigabytes. He
          +re each first-visit return is                                 
       16 +computed from a ring buffer of the last ``W`` costs, where ``g
          +amma^W <= 1e-16``, so later costs                             
       17 +cannot change the return beyond double precision. Memory is O(
          +|S||A| + W) regardless of stream                              
       18 +length, and the results equal the full backward recursion up t
          +o that 1e-16 relative error.                                  
       19  """

      36  def tail_steps(gamma: float, bias: float) -> int:
      37      """Smallest m with gamma**m <= bias * (1 - gamma): steps ne
          eded after the last first visit."""
      38      target = bias * (1 - gamma)
      39 +    if not (0 < gamma < 1 and target > 0):                     
      40 +        raise ValueError("Need 0 < gamma < 1 and bias * (1 - ga
         +mma) > 0")                                                     
      41      m = max(0, math.floor(math.log(target) / math.log(gamma)) -
           1)
      42      while gamma**m > target:
      43          m += 1

       91          support = policy > 0
       92          # Shifting a row of Q by a constant does not change th

       1 +"""Enhancement 3: entropy-regularized SPMD.                    
       2 +                                                               
       3 +The SPMD subproblem gains tau * h(p), h(p) = sum_a p_a log p_a 
         +(proposal Section 2.3):                                        
       4 +                                                               
       5 +    pi_{k+1}(.|s) = argmin_p  eta * ( <Q(s, .), p> + tau * h(p)
         + ) + KL(p || pi_k(.|s))                                        
       6 +                  proportional to pi_k^(1/(1+eta tau)) * exp(-e
         +ta Q / (1 + eta tau)).                                         
       7 +                                                               
       8 +Where the pieces live:                                         
       9 +    closed-form step     ``ie692_rl.core.divergence.KL.step(pol
         +icy, q, eta, tau)`` (tau = 0 is the baseline)                  
      10 +    regularized Q        ``run_spmd(..., tau=...)`` adds tau * 
         +h(pi(.|s)) to every sampled cost at s                          
      11 +    annealed tau         ``AnnealedTau`` below, tau_k = tau_0 /
         + (k + 1)^power                                                 
      12 +    regularized optimum  ``soft_optimal_policy`` below, the pol
         +icy the method converges to                                    
      13 +"""                                                            
      14 +                                                               
      15 +import math                                                    
      16 +from dataclasses import dataclass                              
      17 +                                                               
      18  import numpy as np
      19

        1 -"""Online, fixed-policy, cost-based action-value TD.          
        1 +"""Enhancement 2: online, fixed-policy, cost-based action-valu
          +e TD (lecture notation).                                      
        2 
        3 -All three functions use the lecture notation Q_theta(s,a) = ph
          -i(s,a)^T theta.                                               
        4 -The SAME functions work with tabular and linear Phi from featu
          -res.py.                                                       
        5 -Each result is (Q, observation_count, history), where history 
          -stores                                                        
        6 -(observations, Q_snapshot) at the requested frequency.        
        3 +    Q_theta(s, a) = phi(s, a)^T theta,    delta_t = c_t + gamm
          +a * Q(s_{t+1}, a_{t+1}) - Q(s_t, a_t)                         
        4 +                                                              
        5 +    TD(0)                   theta += eta * delta * phi        
        6 +    TD(lambda)              e = gamma lambda e + phi;         
          +              theta += eta * delta * e                        
        7 +    True Online TD(lambda)  e = gamma lambda e + (1 - eta gamm
          +a lambda e^T phi) phi           (Dutch trace)                 
        8 +                            theta += eta (delta + q - q_old) e
          + - eta (q - q_old) phi                                        
        9 +                            (van Seijen & Sutton, ICML 2014)  
       10 +                                                              
       11 +The next action is sampled from the policy (SARSA-style target
          +); the trace and q_old reset when a                           
       12 +Blackjack hand ends. GridWorld is continuing.                 
       13 +                                                              
       14 +Speed and memory: the features are one-hot (``features.py``), 
          +and an accumulating or Dutch trace                            
       15 +only has non-zero entries for features visited in the last ~lo
          +g(cutoff)/log(gamma lambda) steps.                            
       16 +The kernel keeps those entries in an active list and drops ent
          +ries below ``trace_cutoff``, so a                             
       17 +step costs O(active features) instead of O(d). With cutoff 0 i
          +t is exactly the dense algorithm.                             
       18 +History stores only the estimated V(s0) every ``record_every``
          + steps (not full Q snapshots).                                
       19  """

      164 +def build_method(spec: MethodSpec, params: Params) -> Method: 
      165 +    """Dependency injection: instantiate the estimator and div
          +ergence named in the spec."""                                 
      166 +    groups: dict[str, dict[str, Value]] = {"spmd": {}, "estima
          +tor": {}, "divergence": {}}

      172 +    tau: float | Schedule = AnnealedTau(tau0, float(spmd["tau_
          +power"])) if "tau_power" in spmd else tau0                    
      173 +    return Method(                                            
      174 +        ESTIMATORS[spec.estimator](**groups["estimator"]),  #

      284 +def z_verdict(diff: float, se: float, z_threshold: float, min_
          +worse_diff: float) -> tuple[float, str]:                      
      285 +    """Two-sample z statistic and verdict; se = 0 means a dete
          +rministic difference (z = +-inf)."""                          
      286 +    if not math.isfinite(se):                                 
      287 +        return math.nan, "n/a"                                
      288 +    if se == 0:                                               
      289 +        z = 0.0 if diff == 0 else math.copysign(math.inf, diff
          +)                                                             
      290 +    else:                                                     
      291 +        z = diff / se                                         
      292 +    if z <= -z_threshold:                                     
      293 +        return z, "better"                                    
      294 +    if z >= z_threshold and diff >= min_worse_diff:           
      295 +        return z, "worse"                                     
      296 +    return z, "tie"                                           
      297 +

          +o the last row per seed is the final policy

      307 +        gaps = np.array(list(by_seed.values()))               
      308 +        base = np.array(list(finals.get((problem, baseline), {
          +}).values()))                                                 
      309 +        mean, se = mean_and_se(gaps)                          
      310 +        diff, z, verdict = math.nan, math.nan, ""             
      311 +        if method != baseline and base.size:                  
      312 +            diff = mean - float(base.mean())                  
      313 +            se_diff = math.sqrt(gaps.var(ddof=1) / gaps.size +
          + base.var(ddof=1) / base.size) \                              
      314 +                if gaps.size > 1 and base.size > 1 else math.n
          +an  # fmt: skip                                               
      315 +            z, verdict = z_verdict(diff, se_diff, z_threshold,
          + min_worse_diff)                                              
      316 +        summary.append({"problem": problem, "method": method, 
          +"seeds": int(gaps.size), "mean_final_gap": mean,              
      317 +                        "standard_error": se, "diff_vs_baselin
          +e": diff, "z": z, "verdict": verdict})  # fmt: skip

⏺ The full run failed because Numba's on-disk cache breaks for kernels that take
  other jitted functions as arguments, and my small-scale check missed it by
  using one process. I'll cache only the leaf kernels and compile the
  higher-order ones in memory in each process, starting by measuring that cost. 
  · summary

⏺ The 30x30 TOMC projections are wrong because an unfinished estimate has no
  updates to score, so they should read as infinite and the benchmark should
  fail for those methods. I'll treat the measured --run result as authoritative,
  since TD's 112 s projection still trails the measured 180 s on the mixed-core
  M3. · summary

⏺ The repo is now a pip-installable package, and the full default pipeline runs
  in about 25 s against your 10-minute limit. One exception: on a 30x30 grid,
  the TOMC-based methods can't run in any reasonable time (explained below).
  Lint and strict type checking pass. All 326 default tests pass and 41 skip.
  The skips are the Bregman divergence placeholders, which get tested
  automatically once you fill them in. Nothing is committed; the moves were done
  with git mv, so file history is kept.

  Grid size. The handout (v3 and v4) says 10x10, not 30x30, so as you chose,
  GridWorld(size=N) defaults to 10x10. At 30x30 it has 90 hazards (10% density,
  fixed seed) and goals in the three far corners. Tests cover both sizes.

  Layout
  - src/ie692_rl/ is split three ways:
    - envs/: GridWorld, Blackjack, and the M5 inventory problem, with its data
      file shipped inside the package.
    - core/: TOMC, the SPMD loop, the divergence and estimator base classes, and
      exact scoring.
    - enhancements/: one folder each for bregman_spmd, true_online_td and
      entropy_reg_spmd.
  - tune.py at the root does the grid search and the full runs; all settings are
    in config.cfg.
  - notebooks/ holds the three notebooks, updated to the new code and re-run.
    The entropy notebook took about 2 hours before and now takes 24 s.
  - tests/ has the test suite; benchmarks/bench.py writes results/benchmark.md.

  Bregman divergences (item 12). SPMD now takes its divergence as an argument.
  The base class BregmanDivergence has divergence(p, center) and step(policy, q,
  eta, tau), and KL is the baseline. Euclidean and Tsallis(index) are empty
  placeholders that tune.py skips. Once you implement them,
  tests/test_divergence.py checks them automatically: they must return valid
  policies, never increase expected cost, and actually minimize the SPMD
  subproblem. True Online TD is also plugged into SPMD as an alternative to
  TOMC, as you chose.

  Speed and memory
  - Speed: numba runs the sampling loops about 100x faster than before (roughly
    30M steps/s for TOMC).
  - Memory: peak is under 200 MB per process. The old TOMC stored every step,
    which would be about 10 GB for 10⁸ steps; it now uses a fixed-size buffer,
    so memory no longer grows with run length. The exact models and TD features
    are also much smaller on large grids.
  - Correctness of the rewrite: the new TOMC and TD are tested against
    line-by-line copies of the original algorithms and agree to within 1e-10.
  - The 30x30 limitation: on 30x30, True Online TD runs the full protocol in
    about 3 minutes. TOMC-based methods cannot run there. Under a random policy,
    10⁹ steps still leave 461 of 3600 required pairs unvisited, because hazards
    send the agent back to the start 80% of the time. Extrapolating that trend,
    one estimate wouldn't finish in 10 minutes at any sampling speed. The
    handout allows changing the environment in this case (for example, random
    start states); the 10x10 grid has no such problem.

  Bugs fixed along the way
  - An inventory start_stock larger than capacity was silently mapped to the
    wrong state; it now raises an error.
  - The KL step can no longer produce NaN, even for infinite step sizes or
    extreme Q values.
  - A TD step size that blows up now stops the run with status diverged instead
    of producing NaN policies.
  - Importing the plotting module turned off inline plots in notebooks.
  - A broken line in .gitignore meant neither *.pyc files nor the raw M5 data
    folder were actually ignored.

  Things to know before the report
  - Results won't match earlier numbers exactly. Random actions are now drawn
    differently, so the random sequences differ, even though the algorithms are
    unchanged.
  - Entropy regularization does less than the proposal suggests under TOMC. Once
    an action's probability falls below TOMC's threshold, TOMC assigns it the
    worst-case cost of 10. τ then keeps that probability above zero, but only
    just (around 1e-44 when τ = 0.1).
  - Raw transitions are no longer saved in the baseline notebook. Sampling now
    happens inside compiled code, and a GridWorld run is tens of millions of
    transitions. The notebook saves the per-iteration history and the final
    policy instead.
  - The TD update samples the next action. The proposal's formula uses the
    expected value under the policy instead; I kept your teammates' version and
    noted the difference in the README.

❯ Here is starting code for the Tsallis Bregman Div. You should look into       
  optimizing the binary search                                                  
  https://github.com/jucaleb4/pg-termination/blob/c3673db8d8044d2df99c91c4e7546 
  2ee2228a17f/pg_termination/utils.py#L45                                       
                                                                                
  def tsallis_policy_update(psi, pi, eta, gamma, pi_scratch):                   
      """                                                                       
      :param grad: current gradient estimate                                    
      :param pi: current policy                                                 
      :param eta: step size                                                     
      :param lam: lam to use for warm start                                     
      """                                                                       
      (n_S, n_A) = psi.shape                                                    
      assert n_A == pi.shape[0] and n_S == pi.shape[1], "psi (shape [%d, %d])   
  and pi (shape [%d, %d]) are not transpose to each other" % (n_S, n_A,         
  pi.shape[0], pi.shape[1])                                                     
                                                                                
      tol = 1e-6                                                                
      p = 1/(1 - np.log(1.-gamma)/np.log(2))                                    
                                                                                
      # update state-by-state separately                                        
      for s in range(n_S):                                                      
          g = psi[s,:] + np.power(pi[:,s], p-1)/((1.-p) * eta)                  
          get_x_star = lambda y : np.power(np.maximum(tol, (1.-p) * eta * (g +  
  y)), 1./(p-1))                                                                
          lam = tsallis_update_lambda(get_x_star, tol)                          
          pi_scratch[:,s] = get_x_star(lam)                                     
          pi_sum_s = np.sum(pi_scratch[:,s])                                    
          if pi_sum_s == 0:                                                     
              return False                                                      
          pi_scratch[:,s] /= pi_sum_s                                           
                                                                                
      pi[:,:] = pi_scratch                                                      
      return True                                                               
                                                                                
  def tsallis_update_lambda(get_x_star, tol):                                   
      direction = 0                                                             
                                                                                
      # find whether lam should be positive or negative                         
      u = get_x_star(0)                                                         
      if abs(np.sum(u) - 1) <= tol:                                             
          x_star = u/np.sum(u)                                                  
          return x_star                                                         
      elif np.sum(u) > 1:                                                       
          direction = 1                                                         
      else:                                                                     
          direction = -1                                                        
                                                                                
      # exponential search                                                      
      lam = direction                                                           
      no_exp_search = True                                                      
      u = get_x_star(lam)                                                       
      while (direction == 1 and np.sum(u) > 1) or (direction == -1 and          
  np.sum(u) < 1):                                                               
          no_exp_search = False                                                 
          lam *= 2                                                              
          u = get_x_star(lam)                                                   
                                                                                
      # binary search                                                           
      if direction == 1:                                                        
          lo = 0 if no_exp_search else lam/(2.)                                 
          hi = lam # value that made sum(u) <= 1                                
          while hi-lo > tol:                                                    
              lam = (hi+lo)/2                                                   
              u = get_x_star(lam)                                               
              if abs(np.sum(u) - 1)<=tol: break                                 
              elif np.sum(u) < 1: hi = lam # makes the sum larger               
              else: lo = lam                                                    
          best_lam = hi                                                         
      else:                                                                     
          lo = lam # value that made sum(u) >= 1                                
          hi = 0 if no_exp_search else lam/(2.)                                 
          while hi-lo > tol:                                                    
              lam = (hi+lo)/2                                                   
              u = get_x_star(lam)                                               
              if abs(np.sum(u) - 1)<=tol: break                                 
              elif np.sum(u) < 1: lo = lam                                      
              else: hi = lam                                                    
          best_lam = lo                                                         
                                                                                
      return best_lam                                                           
  We already have the KL divergence function, just move it to the divergences   
  enhancement. For Euclidean, the term is                                       
                                                                                
  def euclidean_bregman(p, q):                                                  
      return 0.5 * ((p - q) ** 2).sum(axis=-1)

      28 +        self.check_step_inputs(policy, q, eta, tau)            
      29 +        support = policy > 0                                   
      30 +        # Shifting a row of Q by a constant does not change the
         + update; it keeps eta * Q bounded                              
      31 +        # for the best action. Weights are written so that eta 
         += inf or eta * tau = inf stay defined.                         
      32 +        eta_tau = 0.0 if tau == 0 else eta * tau               
      33 +        weight_old = 1.0 / (1.0 + eta_tau)  # exponent on pi_k 
      34 +        weight_q = eta if tau == 0 else 1.0 / (1.0 / eta + tau)
         +  # eta / (1 + eta tau)

      37 +            logits = np.where(

         +policy, 1.0)) - weight_q * shifted, -np.inf

⏺ Euclidean and Tsallis are next, with a shared base-class helper that computes
  η·(Q − row min) safely when η is infinite. · summary

⏺ Bash(python3 - <<'EOF'

          gative and finite")

      60 +    @staticmethod                                              
      61 +    def scaled_costs(q: FloatArray, eta: float) -> FloatArray: 
      62 +        """eta * (Q - row minimum): non-negative, 0 at each row
         +'s best actions even for eta = inf.                            
      63 +                                                               
      64 +        Shifting a row of Q by a constant never changes an SPMD
         + step (the simplex constraint absorbs                          
      65 +        it), and the shift keeps eta * Q from overflowing for t
         +he actions that matter.                                        
      66 +        """                                                    
      67 +        with np.errstate(over="ignore", invalid="ignore"):     
      68 +            shifted = q - q.min(axis=1, keepdims=True)         
      69 +            scaled: FloatArray = np.where(shifted > 0, eta * sh
         +ifted, 0.0)

      32 +def project_to_simplex(v: FloatArray) -> FloatArray:           
      33 +    """Row-wise argmin_{p in simplex} ||p - v||^2 (Duchi, Shale
         +v-Shwartz, Singer & Chandra, 2008)."""                         
      34 +    n_rows, n_cols = v.shape

      38 +    last = n_cols - 1 - np.argmax(positive[:, ::-1], axis=1)  #

⏺ I've implemented the Tsallis and Euclidean steps, moved KL into the Bregman
  divergences enhancement, and rewritten the Tsallis search. Your starting code
  turned out to have a bug that gives wrong policies, so read that part first.

  Bug in the Tsallis starting code. When the solution needs a negative
  multiplier, which happens often, the binary search moves the wrong end of its
  interval when the sum is below 1. It then ends up outside the valid region,
  where the max(tol, ·) clamp creates huge entries. For example, the policy
  [0.39, 0.59, 0.01, 0.001] became [0.5, 0.5, 0, 0], which is worse on the
  objective than not moving at all. A second, smaller bug: when the starting sum
  is already within tolerance, it returns the policy row instead of the
  multiplier. I kept a fixed copy in tests/reference.py to test against. With
  both bugs fixed it agrees with the new code to about 1e-4 (its own tolerance),
  and the new code is never worse on the objective.

  The faster search (enhancements/bregman_spmd/tsallis.py):
  - No exponential search: the interval containing the root can be written down
    directly from the policy and Q.
  - Newton instead of bisection: the slope of the sum has a simple formula, so
    each step converges much faster. A bisection step is used only if Newton
    would leave the interval.
  - Warm start: a first-order estimate of the multiplier is nearly exact for
    small step sizes.
  - Compiled with numba, with no clamping.

  It reaches full machine precision in 3–10 iterations per state (3–6 for your
  index of 0.231), against 1e-6 for the old search. It runs 30–130x faster: 0.6
  ms instead of 65 ms per update on a 30x30 grid. index_from_gamma(γ) reproduces
  the index from your code, about 0.231 at γ = 0.9.

  Euclidean uses your divergence formula. Its step is an exact projection of π −
  ηQ onto the simplex, computed for all states at once with no search. As
  checks that the three divergences are consistent, Tsallis with index 2 matches
  Euclidean to within 1e-13, and an index near 1 matches KL.

  What's left out: entropy regularization (τ > 0) only has a closed form for KL.
  Euclidean and Tsallis raise NotImplementedError if τ > 0 is passed, so the
  "best divergence + entropy" combination would need an extra numerical solve.

  config.cfg grids, chosen from a quick wider search:
  - Euclidean: step size 0.5, 5, 50 × bias.
  - Tsallis: step size 0.3, 3, 30 × index 0.2314, 0.5, 3.5. Both are tuned
    because the useful step size depends strongly on the index: indices below 1
    want large steps, 3.5 wants small ones.

  Results so far. The default tune.py now runs all six methods in 28 s, with a
  211 MB peak. On GridWorld, Tsallis ties the baseline (gap 0.087 vs 0.142, z =
  −1.2), as does Euclidean (0.160, z = +0.3). True Online TD is still the only
  method clearly better than the baseline.

  Blackjack results are not usable yet. Every method that uses TOMC finishes
  with exactly the same gap, 0.0872. Tuning on 10% of 120k steps allows only
  about 2 updates, so the search picks the largest step size. That jumps
  straight to the greedy policy, which is the same for every divergence. A
  larger Blackjack budget or tuning fraction is needed before the report
  compares divergences there; I've noted this in the README.

  All 437 tests pass (the 41 earlier skips now run on the real divergences), and
  lint and strict type checks pass. I re-ran the two notebooks whose imports
  changed and updated the README. Nothing is committed.

  - Outlier seeds drive the averages. Seeds stuck with a gap above 0.2 number 2
    of 20 for KL, 4 of 20 for Euclidean and 1 of 20 for Tsallis. Those stuck
    seeds explain KL's and Euclidean's wide error bands; median gaps are 0.102
    (KL), 0.096 (Euclidean) and 0.072 (Tsallis).
  - Tsallis makes far fewer, longer updates. It averages a median of 8 updates
    per run against about 33 for KL. My guess (I haven't measured it) is that
    the index-0.5 barrier keeps more actions above TOMC's coverage threshold, so
    each estimate needs a longer rollout.
  - Index 0.5 was chosen over pg-termination's 0.231, which came second at 0.088
    in tuning.

⏺ The policy's value never moves after the first update, so the lock-in comes

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ A bigger budget alone doesn't fix Blackjack, but more tuning seeds do: with
  them, Tsallis clearly beats KL. All of this ran from scratch copies of the
  config, so config.cfg is unchanged.

  Bigger budget only (3 tuning seeds): at 1.2M and at 12M steps, every
  divergence still ends at exactly 0.0872 ± 0.0221, the same as at 120k.

  - Why: the policy freezes after its first update. In the full runs, its exact
    value changed a median of 0 times across about 39,000 updates. A large step
    makes the policy greedy at once. TOMC then gives every action below π_min
    the pessimistic value 10, so nothing can move it again. Each seed ends
    either at the optimum or stuck at a gap of 0.194, decided by its first
    estimate.
  - Why tuning can't see it: with 3 tuning seeds, every combination scores
    either 0 (all three lucky) or 0.0646 (one stuck). The grid search is
    effectively a coin flip that tends to pick the largest step.

  10x budget (1.2M) with 20 tuning seeds (100–119, separate from the 20 test 
  seeds): the methods now separate.

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │  Divergence  │ Chosen settings  │ Final gap (20 test   │     vs KL      │
  │              │                  │        seeds)        │                │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ KL           │ η = 1, bias =    │ 0.097 ± 0.022        │                │
  │ (baseline)   │ 0.003            │                      │                │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────┤

  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Tsallis      │ η = 0.3, index   │ 0.000 ± 0.000        │ better (z =    │
  │              │ 0.5              │                      │ −4.4)          │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────────┴────────────────┘

  Tsallis with index 0.5 and a small step reached the optimal policy on all 20
  test seeds. My explanation, which I haven't tested separately: an index below
  1 is a barrier that keeps every action's probability positive, so no action
  drops below TOMC's coverage threshold and gets locked out. KL also chose a
  small step, but its test result (0.097) is much worse than its tuning score
  (0.040), so with 20 tuning seeds the scores are still noisy.

  Should I make this the real setup? That means a Blackjack budget of 1.2M in
  config.cfg, 20 tuning seeds, and rerunning GridWorld and Blackjack into
  results/bregman/. The tuning seeds are one setting shared by both problems, so
  GridWorld tuning would take about 7x longer. The whole pipeline would still
  finish in about a minute. The alternative is a per-problem seed override that
  only gives Blackjack 20 seeds, which is a small code change.

✻ Brewed for 2m 46s · done 1:55 PM

※ recap: We're tuning the Bregman divergences on GridWorld and Blackjack, and with more tuning seeds Tsallis clearly beats KL on Blackjack. Next, please say whether to make the 1.2M budget and 20 tuning seeds permanent and rerun both problems. (turn off recaps in /config)

❯ Do we have an existing method in the code that is independent of the true Vpi 
  (dont need access to P matrix) for estimating the convergence? Or for         
  estimating optimality?                                                        

⏺ No. Every convergence or optimality number the code reports, including the gap
  used to pick hyperparameters in tune.py, comes from ExactScorer, which uses
  the true transition model. What exists without the model:

  Quantity: estimated_value: V̂(s₀) from the current TOMC/TD estimate
  Where: run_spmd history, every iteration
  Model-free?: yes
  What it tells you: progress, but noisy and biased by the pessimistic fill;
    not an optimality measure
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Quantity: min_prob, states_visited
  Where: run_spmd history
  Model-free?: yes
  What it tells you: collapse and coverage diagnostics, not convergence
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Quantity: Independent Monte Carlo rollouts of V^π(s₀)
  Where: validate_start_value in notebooks/td_evaluation.ipynb
  Model-free?: yes
  What it tells you: unbiased value of one fixed policy; says nothing about
    how far it is from optimal
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Quantity: exact_value, gap, and the tuning criterion mean_final_gap
  Where: ExactScorer
  Model-free?: no
  What it tells you: the true gap to optimal

  So the protocol currently picks hyperparameters with model knowledge that an
  online learner wouldn't have. That's fine as an evaluation tool, but worth
  saying in the report.

  What could be added: an advantage-gap bound. It needs only Q^π, which our
  estimators already produce for every pair. With costs, define for each state

    g(s) = V^π(s) − min_a Q^π(s, a) ≥ 0, where V^π(s) = Σ_a π(a|s) Q^π(s, a).

  The performance-difference lemma, together with Q^π ≥ Q*, gives two-sided
  bounds on the true gap:

    g(s₀) ≤ V^π(s₀) − V*(s₀) ≤ max_s g(s) / (1 − γ).

  When g = 0 everywhere, π is optimal, so this works both as a stopping rule and
  as a model-free certificate. Your Tsallis starting code comes from a
  repository called pg-termination, which I believe is built around this kind of
  criterion; I haven't checked it.

  Caveat: TOMC's Q̂ can't be plugged in directly. Pairs with π(a|s) < π_min get
  the pessimistic value 10, so if π has abandoned a good action, the min over
  actions misses it. g then looks like 0 and would falsely certify optimality.
  That is exactly the Blackjack lock-in we just saw. A reliable estimate needs Q̂
  for every action, for example from a separate validation pass that forces
  each action once per state and then follows π. It also needs a noise margin,
  because the max over states of noisy estimates is biased upward.

  Should I implement it as advantage_gap(q, policy) plus a validation estimator,
  with tests that check the bounds against ExactScorer on all three problems?
  run_spmd could then record it every iteration, and tune.py could optionally
  select hyperparameters with it instead of the exact gap.

✻ Cogitated for 37s · done 2:01 PM

❯ yes implement the advantage gap with tests                                    

⏺ I'll implement the bounds, a model-free validation estimator for Q^π, and
  hooks into run_spmd. One design decision to flag first. An unbiased Q̂ for
  every action, including ones the policy has abandoned, needs rollouts that
  start from each (s, a). That uses the simulator's ability to step from any
  state (a generative model): no P matrix, but more than a single on-policy
  stream. Every one of our environments supports this through step(state, 
  action). Validation samples will be counted separately from the training
  budget and drawn from their own random stream, so certifying a run doesn't
  change its training trajectory.

       4 
       5     g(s) = V(s) - min_a Q(s, a)  >= 0.
       6 
       7 Two facts bound the true gap to the optimum without knowing V*:
       8 
       9   * lower bound: Q^pi >= Q* gives min_a Q^pi(s, a) >= V*(s), so
     … +146 lines (~7 KB) (ctrl+o to expand)

      166 +## Model-free optimality certificate (`core/optimality.py`)

      168 +The exact gap needs the model P. The **advantage gap** g(s) =

      169 +Q^π. Since Q^π ≥ Q* and by the performance-difference lemma,

      190  ## Experiments and tuning protocol
      191

⏺ The advantage-gap certificate is in src/ie692_rl/core/optimality.py with tests
  in tests/test_optimality.py, and it estimates optimality without the P
  matrix. All 483 tests pass, lint and strict type checks are clean, and nothing
  is committed yet.

  What it computes. For a policy π, the advantage gap at each state is g(s) =
  V^π(s) − min_a Q^π(s,a). It bounds the true optimality gap from both sides:
  g(s₀) ≤ V^π(s₀) − V*(s₀) ≤ max_s g(s) / (1 − γ), and g = 0 everywhere exactly
  when π is optimal.

  How to use it.
  - From any Q, exact or estimated: advantage_gap(q, policy) returns g, and
    gap_bounds(q, policy, gamma, start, q_se, z) returns both bounds, widened by
    z standard errors when you pass q_se.
  - Model-free: RolloutCertifier().certify(env, policy, rng) estimates Q^π for
    every state and action by trying each action once and then following π. It
    needs only the simulator, started from arbitrary states, never P. It does
    many more rollouts at the start state, where the lower bound is read. Cost
    is 0.2–0.7 s per certificate, including on the 30x30 grid.
  - In training: run_spmd(..., certifier=..., certify_every=k) adds gap_lower,
    gap_upper and validation_observations to the history. Validation uses its
    own random stream and isn't charged to the training budget, so the training
    run is identical with or without it; a test checks this.

  How good the bounds are:
  - Validity: the rollout bounds bracketed the true gap on every test policy
    across all four problems.
  - Lower bound: usually informative. For a random GridWorld policy it reads
    0.005, against 0.0195 with exact Q.
  - Upper bound: valid but very loose, typically 5–50x the true gap, so treat it
    as a sanity check rather than a target.

  The Blackjack lock-in, caught. The deterministic policy "draw below 7" has a
  true gap of 0.194, the same value the stuck seeds reached earlier. Bounds
  computed from TOMC's own Q-hat say [0, 0], i.e. "optimal". That's because
  every action the policy never takes gets the pessimistic value 10. The rollout
  certifier gives a lower bound of 0.10, correctly flagging it, and returns 0
  for the truly optimal thresholds (draw below 9 or 10). One consequence: never
  compute the certificate from TOMC's training estimates.

  Not done yet: tune.py still selects hyperparameters with the exact,
  model-based gap. Next steps I can take:
  - Add an option to select hyperparameters by the certifier's lower bound
    instead, so tuning never touches the model.
  - Commit and push this.

✻ Brewed for 5m 24s · done 2:07 PM

❯ Select hyperparams using the lower bound (from advantage gap). If tests (for  
  changed areas) pass then commit and push.                                     

⏺ Before wiring this in, one concern about selecting on the current lower bound
  g(s₀): it only measures how much better a single action change at s₀ could do.
  For a random GridWorld policy it reads 0.0195 while the true gap is 0.36, and
  it is often exactly 0, so many combinations would tie and the choice would be
  arbitrary.

  There is a tighter bound that follows from the same advantage gap. Take π′
  greedy with respect to Q^π. The performance-difference lemma gives V^π(s₀) −
  V^{π′}(s₀) = E_{d^{π′}}[g]/(1−γ), which is at least g(s₀). It is also at most
  V^π(s₀) − V*(s₀), because V* ≤ V^{π′} for any π′. So it is still a valid lower
  bound even when π′ comes from a noisy Q̂, and it can be estimated by rollouts
  of π and π′ from s₀. I'll compare the two with exact values first:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ On GridWorld the improvement bound (0.23–0.31) is tighter than g(s₀) and close
  to the true gap, while Blackjack matches and Inventory and 30x30 are nearly
  exact. I'm making the certifier's lower bound the larger of the two, each with
  its own margin, and documenting both formulas in the module docs. · summary

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Wiring is done: a selection setting (defaulting to gap_lower) and the
  certifier settings live in config.cfg, every run's final policy gets
  certified, and tuning rows record mean lower and upper bounds. Combinations
  are chosen by lowest mean lower bound, with ties going to the upper bound,
  while the exact gap is still reported for evaluation. · summary

      171    g(s₀) ≤ V^π(s₀) − V*(s₀) ≤ max_s g(s) / (1 − γ),
      172 
      173 -and g = 0 everywhere exactly when π is optimal. The lower boun
          -d is usually informative; the upper                           
      174 -bound is loose (the 1/(1−γ) factor and the max over states).  
      173 +and g = 0 everywhere exactly when π is optimal. The upper boun
          +d is loose (the 1/(1−γ) factor and                            
      174 +the max over states).                                         
      175 +                                                              
      176 +**A tighter lower bound** follows from the same gap. Let π′ be
          + greedy for Q^π. By the                                       
      177 +performance-difference lemma, V^π(s₀) − V^{π′}(s₀) = E_{d^{π′}
          +}[g]/(1−γ), which is at least g(s₀).                          
      178 +And since V* ≤ V^{π′} for any π′, it is at most the true gap, 
          +even when π′ comes from a noisy Q̂.                            
      179 +                                                              
      180 +| Random policy | true gap | g(s₀) | V^π − V^greedy |         
      181 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      182 +| GridWorld 10×10 | 0.36 | 0.02 | 0.31 |                      
      183 +| Inventory | 6.1 | 0.49 | 5.7 |                              
      184 +| Blackjack | 0.25 | 0.06 | 0.06 |                            
      185 +                                                              
      186 +The certifier's lower bound is the larger of the two estimated
          + bounds.                                                      
      187 
      188  - `advantage_gap(q, policy)` computes g, and `gap_bounds(q, po
           licy, gamma, start, q_se, z)` returns
      189    both bounds from exact or estimated Q, with z-standard-error
            margins on the action differences.
      190  - `RolloutCertifier(n_rollouts, n_start_rollouts, z).certify(e
           nv, policy, rng)` estimates Q^π for
      191    every (s, a): take a, then follow π, until γ^t < 1e-16. It u
           ses the simulator only (no P), started
      192    in arbitrary states, with many more rollouts at the start st
           ate, where the lower bound is read.
      193 +  It then rolls out π and the greedy π′ from the start state f
          +or the improvement bound. Each z-level                        
      194 +  margin is applied separately, and `lower = max(gap_lower, im
          +provement_lower)`. A certificate takes                        
      195 +  about 0.2 s on GridWorld and 0.8 s on 30×30.                
      196  - `run_spmd(..., certifier=RolloutCertifier(), certify_every=k
           )` adds `gap_lower`, `gap_upper` and
      197    `validation_observations` to the history. These run on their
            own random stream and are not
      198    charged to the training budget, so training is unchanged.

⏺ Lint, formatting, strict mypy and the 117 affected tests all pass, and the
  Bregman grid results in results/bregman/ are staged with the code. I'm
  checking whether the remote has moved before pushing. · summary

  Pushed to main, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Hyperparameters are now selected using the lower bound, which never touches
  the P matrix. All tests passed: 498 in the default suite plus the 9 perf and
  slow tests, with lint and strict type checks clean. The change is committed as
  69bbf70 and pushed to origin/main.

  Change to the lower bound. The bound I described before, g(s₀), turned out too
  weak to choose with. On a random GridWorld policy it reads 0.02 while the
  true gap is 0.36, so most combinations would tie at 0. I added a tighter bound
  built from the same advantage gap: V^π(s₀) − V^{π′}(s₀), where π′ is greedy
  with respect to Q^π. It is always at least g(s₀) and never more than the true
  gap, even when π′ comes from a noisy estimate. On the same GridWorld policy it
  reads 0.31. The certifier reports the larger of the two bounds.

  How selection works.
  - Certification: every run's final policy is certified with simulator
    rollouts. These are not charged to the training budget and don't change
    training.
  - Choice: each method gets the combination with the smallest mean lower bound;
    ties go to the smallest upper bound.
  - Settings: this is now the default (selection = gap_lower in config.cfg).
    --selection exact_gap brings back the old model-based choice.
  - Reporting: the final comparison between methods still uses the exact gap,
    and summary.csv now also shows the mean lower bound.

  Bregman results with the new selection (results/bregman/):
  - Settings chosen: the same GridWorld settings as exact-gap selection for KL
    and Tsallis. For Euclidean it chose rollout length (bias) 0.03 instead of
    0.003.
  - Ranking: the same as the exact gap. Tsallis is best by both measures, with
    an exact gap of 0.087 and a lower bound of 0.019, but still only ties KL
    statistically.
  - Weakness: for nearly converged policies the bound is loose, about
    0.001–0.007 against an exact gap of about 0.075, so it mostly rules out
    clearly bad settings.
  - Blackjack is still uninformative: its budget and tuning seeds haven't
    changed.

  Cost. Certifying every final policy raises the default pipeline from about 28
  s to 48 s, still well inside the 10-minute limit. results/benchmark.md has
  been regenerated.

  Still open: Blackjack. The earlier test showed that 20 tuning seeds and a
  larger budget let the divergences separate. Tsallis reached the optimal policy
  on all 20 test seeds and beat KL (z = −4.4). I can make that the configured
  setup and regenerate the Bregman results.

✻ Baked for 11m 7s · done 2:20 PM

※ recap: We're comparing Bregman divergences (KL, Euclidean, Tsallis) for your IE 692 project, and hyperparameter selection now uses the model-free lower bound, committed and pushed. Next: confirm the 1.2M Blackjack budget with 20 tuning seeds, then rerun. (turn off recaps in /config)

❯ We should plot the advantage gap as the second row of plots in the results    
  plots. Do we currently do so?                                                 

⏺ No. learning_curves.png currently has two rows:

  1. the exact gap of the current policy against environment steps;
  2. the latest estimated V(s₀), averaged over seeds.

  The advantage gap isn't plotted anywhere. It also couldn't be yet: full runs
  only certify the final policy, so history.csv has one bound per run, not a
  curve. The only plot that uses it is the tuning plot's y-axis (the mean lower
  bound per combination).

  Making it the second row needs two changes:

  - Record it along each run. run_spmd can already certify every k-th update,
    but that would be costly. A certificate takes about 0.2 s on GridWorld and
    0.8 s on 30x30, and True Online TD makes about 400 updates per run, so
    per-update certification would add about 80 s per GridWorld TD run. Instead,
    I'd certify at fixed budget checkpoints, say 20 points between 0% and 100%
    of the observation budget. That costs about 4 s per GridWorld run regardless
    of method, so the default pipeline would go from about 48 s to roughly
    1.5–2 minutes on 7 workers. The number of checkpoints would be a config.cfg
    setting.
  - Plot it. Insert a new second row showing the mean certified lower bound
    against environment steps, with a shaded ±1 SE band, one line per method.
    The upper bound is too loose to plot usefully; it is 5–50x the true gap. The
    exact gap stays in row 1, so the two can be compared directly.

  The estimated V(s₀) row would move to row 3 rather than being replaced. The
  handout requires reporting the estimated value V^{πₜ}(s) at each iteration, so
  it has to stay. Should I go ahead with 20 checkpoints?

      38 
      39  `tune.py` writes the following to `results/tuned/`:
      40 
      41 -- `tuning.csv` and `tuning.png`: every grid-search combination 
      41 +- `tuning.csv` and `tuning_<problem>.png`: every grid-search co
         +mbination                                                      
      42  - `history.csv`: every iteration of every full run, with estima
          ted V(s₀), observations, exact gap,
      43    smallest action probability and states visited
      44  - `summary.csv`: final gap per method with a z-test against the
           baseline
      45 -- `learning_curves.png`: exact gap and estimated V(s₀) against 
         -observations                                                   
      45 +- `learning_curves_<problem>.png`: mean exact optimality gap ag
         +ainst environment steps                                        
      46 
      47  All settings are in `config.cfg`.
      48 
      49 +Figures are drawn one per problem. Each title is the problem's 
         +display name and each legend entry a                           
      50 +method's display name, both set by the `label` keys in `config.
         +cfg`; descriptions belong in figure                            
      51 +captions. Math is typeset with matplotlib's mathtext in STIX fo
         +nts, which accepts LaTeX syntax                                
      52 +(`$V^{\pi}(s_0)$`) without a TeX installation.                 
      53 +                                                               
      54  ## Problem setup
      55 
      56  Every problem minimizes discounted **cost** with γ = 0.9 and ru
          ns in the **online** model: learners
     ...
      165  sampled cost at state s then includes τ Σ_a π(a|s) log π(a|s),
            so the estimator returns the
      166  regularized Q. `KL().step(..., tau)` applies the closed form
      167  π ∝ π^{1/(1+ητ)} exp(−ηQ/(1+ητ)). `soft_optimal_policy` gives
           the regularized optimum the method
      163 -converges to. Its estimated V(s₀) includes the (negative) entr
          -opy term, so it sits below the other                          
      164 -methods' estimates in `learning_curves.png`; the exact gap is 
          -computed without regularization.                              
      168 +converges to. Its estimated V(s₀) in `history.csv` includes th
          +e (negative) entropy term; the exact                          
      169 +gap is computed without regularization.

⏺ I've reworked both report figures. The estimated V(s₀) row is gone and each
  problem now gets its own file. Nothing is committed yet. All 500 tests pass,
  and lint and strict type checks are clean.

  1. Capitalization: titles and axis labels are now in title case, e.g.
     "Environment Steps (Millions)" and "Optimality Gap $V^{\pi_t}(s_0) -
     V^{\star}(s_0)$".
  2. Legend names: every method and problem section in config.cfg has a label
     key, which can contain LaTeX math. The current labels are "KL (Baseline)",
     "Entropy-Regularized KL", "Annealed Entropy-Regularized KL", "True Online
     TD($\lambda$)", "Euclidean", "Tsallis", "GridWorld ($10 \times 10$)" and
     "Blackjack". Without a label, the section name is used.
  3. One file per problem: learning_curves_<problem>.png and
     tuning_<problem>.png. --plot-only redraws them from the saved CSVs.
  4. LaTeX: math is set with matplotlib's built-in LaTeX-style renderer
     (mathtext) in STIX fonts, so text and symbols match. This needs no TeX
     installation on your teammates' machines. LaTeX is installed on yours, so I
     can switch to full usetex rendering if you'd prefer it.
  5. Titles: each title is just the problem's display name; descriptions such as
     "(mean over seeds)" are gone.
  6. Axes:
     - Step counts are labelled with their unit ("Thousands" or "Millions")
       instead of a "1e7" offset in the corner.
     - Ticks fall on round steps (0.05, 0.1, …).
     - The learning-curve y-axis runs from 0 to just above the shared starting
       gap, so the initial drop and the late gaps between methods both stay
       visible.
     - In the tuning plots, combinations at exactly 0 are no longer hidden by
       the axis line, and method names are rotated only when there are more than
       three.

          -e top-left corner (see `figures/gridworld_problem.png`). The i
          -ntended move happens with probability 0.8; otherwise one of th
          -e other three directions is taken uniformly, and walls clip mo
          -vement. The cost is charged at the current cell: 0 at a goal, 
          -1 at a hazard, 0.2 elsewhere. From a goal or hazard the agent

          - new hand is dealt after each finished one. Values are reporte
