       65  ```
       66 
       94 -**Baseline.** `run_spmd(env, TOMC(bias), KL(), step_size, ...)
          -`. Each iteration runs one TOMC                               
       95 -estimate. The on-policy stream continues until every pair with
          - π(a|s) ≥ (1−γ)/|A| has been visited                          
       96 -*and* the discounted tail after the last first visit is below 
          -`bias·(1−γ)`. Q(s, a) is the return                           
       97 -after the first visit; pairs that are not required get the pes
          -simistic `cost_bound/(1−γ)`. Then the                         
       98 -KL step is applied: π ← π·exp(−ηQ), normalized.               
       67 +`run_spmd(env, estimator, divergence, step_size, budget, seed,
          + tau)` takes its policy evaluator and                         
       68 +its Bregman divergence as objects. The baseline is `TOMC` + `K
          +L`, and each enhancement swaps one part:                      
       69 
      100 -**Dependency injection.** `run_spmd` receives its policy evalu
          -ator and its divergence as objects:                           
      101 -                                                              
      102 -- `QEstimator.estimate(env, policy, rng, max_observations, ext
          -ra_cost)` returns `(Q, observations, states_visited)`.        
      103 -- `BregmanDivergence.step(policy, q, eta, tau)` returns the ne
          -w policy, and `divergence(p, center)` returns D(p, center) per
          - state.                                                       
      104 -                                                              
      105 -The SPMD subproblem is `argmin_p η(⟨Q, p⟩ + τ h(p)) + D(p, π_k
          -)`, where h(p) = Σ p log p.                                   
      106 -`config.cfg` picks the classes by name (`estimator = tomc | tr
          -ue_online_td`,                                                
      107 -`divergence = kl | euclidean | tsallis`) and passes `estimator
          -.*` / `divergence.*` keys to their                            
      108 -constructors.                                                 
      109 -                                                              
      110 -### Enhancement 1: SPMD with three Bregman divergences (`enhan
          -cements/bregman_spmd/`)                                       
      111 -                                                              
      112 -The three divergences are in `enhancements/bregman_spmd/`; KL 
          -is also the baseline's step. Each                             
      113 -subclasses `BregmanDivergence` and is selected with `divergenc
          -e = kl | euclidean | tsallis`.                                
      114 -                                                              
      115 -| Divergence | w(p) | Step | Notes |                          
      116 -|---|---|---|---|                                             
      117 -| `KL()` | Σ p log p | closed form π ∝ π^{1/(1+ητ)} exp(−ηQ/(1
          -+ητ)) | the only one with τ > 0 (enhancement 3) |             
      118 -| `Euclidean()` | ½‖p‖² | exact projection of π − ηQ onto the 
          -simplex (sort-based, Duchi et al. 2008) | actions can reach ex
          -actly 0 and come back |                                       
      119 -| `Tsallis(index)` | (Σ p^q − 1)/(q(q−1)) | per-state root-fin
          -ding for the KKT multiplier (below) | q → 1 is KL, q = 2 is Eu
          -clidean; q < 1 is a barrier |                                 
      120 -                                                              
      121 -**Tsallis solver.** The step is x_a(λ) = ((q−1)(c_a − λ))^{1/(
          -q−1)} with c_a = π_a^{q−1}/(q−1) − ηQ_a,                      
      122 -where λ solves Σ x_a(λ) = 1. Our starting code (pg-termination
          -'s `tsallis_policy_update`) searches                          
      123 -for a bracket exponentially, then bisects to 1e-6 in Python. T
          -he new solver:                                                
      124 -                                                              
      125 -- **Exact bracket, no search:** f(lo) ≥ 0 ≥ f(hi), with lo = m
          -ax c − 1/(q−1) and hi = max c − n^{1−q}/(q−1).                
      126 -- **Safeguarded Newton:** f′(λ) = −Σ x^{2−q} is known in close
          -d form, so steps converge                                     
      127 -  quadratically; a step that leaves the bracket falls back to 
          -bisection.                                                    
      128 -- **Warm start** from the first-order solution λ₀ = −Σ π^{2−q}
          -ηQ / Σ π^{2−q}.                                               
      129 -- **Compiled with numba,** with no clamping.                  
      130 -                                                              
      131 -It reaches machine precision in 3–10 iterations per state and 
          -runs 30–130× faster than the reference                        
      132 -(0.6 ms instead of 65 ms for a 30×30 grid). `index_from_gamma(
          -γ)` gives pg-termination's index,                             
      133 -1/(1 − log(1−γ)/log 2) ≈ 0.231 for γ = 0.9.                   
      134 -                                                              
      135 -**Two bugs in the starting code** are documented and fixed in 
          -the port used as a test oracle                                
      136 -(`tests/reference.py`):                                       
      137 -                                                              
      138 -- When the initial sum is within tolerance, `tsallis_update_la
          -mbda` returns the policy row instead                          
      139 -  of the multiplier.                                          
      140 -- For a negative multiplier, the bisection swaps `lo` and `hi`
          -, so it converges to the far end of the                       
      141 -  bracket. That point is outside the domain, where the 1e-6 cl
          -amp creates huge entries; for example                         
      142 -  [0.39, 0.59, 0.01, 0.001] became [0.5, 0.5, 0, 0], worse on 
          -the objective than not moving.                                
      143 -                                                              
      144 -With both fixed, it agrees with the new solver to about 1e-4 (
          -its tolerance), and the new solver is                         
      145 -never worse on the objective.                                 
      146 -                                                              
      147 -**Tests.** The contract tests in `tests/test_divergence.py` ru
          -n on every divergence: D(p, p) = 0,                           
      148 -D ≥ 0, steps are probability vectors, `step` minimizes the sub
          -problem, small steps barely move,                             
      149 -expected cost never rises, huge or infinite steps are greedy, 
          -and extreme inputs stay finite.                               
      150 -Specific checks cover Tsallis(2) = Euclidean, Tsallis(1 ± 1e-6
          -) ≈ KL, and agreement with the                                
      151 -reference.                                                    
      152 -                                                              
      153 -### Enhancement 2: True Online TD(λ) (`enhancements/true_onlin
          -e_td/`)                                                       
      154 -                                                              
      155 -`TrueOnlineTD(eta, lam, n_steps, features)` is a `QEstimator`.
          - Each SPMD iteration uses `n_steps`                           
      156 -fresh on-policy transitions with a Dutch trace and the true-on
          -line correction (van Seijen & Sutton,                         
      157 -2014). Features are one-hot (`tabular`, or `linear` = 2×2-bloc
          -k or total-band state aggregation).                           
      158 -`td_zero`, `td_lambda` and `true_online_td_lambda` run a fixed
          --policy comparison                                            
      159 -(`notebooks/td_evaluation.ipynb`). As in the original code, th
          -e target samples the next action                              
      160 -(SARSA style); the proposal's formula uses the expectation ove
          -r π instead.                                                  
      161 -                                                              
      162 -### Enhancement 3: Entropy-regularized SPMD (`enhancements/ent
          -ropy_reg_spmd/`)                                              
      163 -                                                              
      164 -Use `run_spmd(..., tau=τ)` with a fixed τ, or `tau=AnnealedTau
          -(τ₀, p)` for τ_k = τ₀/(k+1)^p. Each                           
      165 -sampled cost at state s then includes τ Σ_a π(a|s) log π(a|s),
          - so the estimator returns the                                 
      166 -regularized Q. `KL().step(..., tau)` applies the closed form  
      167 -π ∝ π^{1/(1+ητ)} exp(−ηQ/(1+ητ)). `soft_optimal_policy` gives 
          -the regularized optimum the method                            
      168 -converges to. Its estimated V(s₀) in `history.csv` includes th
          -e (negative) entropy term; the exact                          
      169 -gap is computed without regularization.                       
      170 -                                                              
      171 -## Model-free optimality certificate (`core/optimality.py`)   
      172 -                                                              
      173 -The exact gap needs the model P. The **advantage gap** g(s) = 
          -V^π(s) − min_a Q^π(s, a) needs only                           
      174 -Q^π. Since Q^π ≥ Q* and by the performance-difference lemma,  
      175 -                                                              
      176 -  g(s₀) ≤ V^π(s₀) − V*(s₀) ≤ max_s g(s) / (1 − γ),            
      177 -                                                              
      178 -and g = 0 everywhere exactly when π is optimal. The upper boun
          -d is loose (the 1/(1−γ) factor and                            
      179 -the max over states).                                         
      180 -                                                              
      181 -**A tighter lower bound** follows from the same gap. Let π′ be
          - greedy for Q^π. By the                                       
      182 -performance-difference lemma, V^π(s₀) − V^{π′}(s₀) = E_{d^{π′}
          -}[g]/(1−γ), which is at least g(s₀).                          
      183 -And since V* ≤ V^{π′} for any π′, it is at most the true gap, 
          -even when π′ comes from a noisy Q̂.                            
      184 -                                                              
      185 -| Random policy | true gap | g(s₀) | V^π − V^greedy |         
      186 -|---|---|---|---|                                             
      187 -| GridWorld 10×10 | 0.36 | 0.02 | 0.31 |                      
      188 -| Inventory | 6.1 | 0.49 | 5.7 |                              
      189 -| Blackjack | 0.25 | 0.06 | 0.06 |                            
      190 -                                                              
      191 -The certifier's lower bound is the larger of the two estimated
          - bounds.                                                      
      192 -                                                              
      193 -- `advantage_gap(q, policy)` computes g, and `gap_bounds(q, po
          -licy, gamma, start, q_se, z)` returns                         
      194 -  both bounds from exact or estimated Q, with z-standard-error
          - margins on the action differences.                           
      195 -- `RolloutCertifier(n_rollouts, n_start_rollouts, z).certify(e
          -nv, policy, rng)` estimates Q^π for                           
      196 -  every (s, a): take a, then follow π, until γ^t < 1e-16. It u
          -ses the simulator only (no P), started                        
      197 -  in arbitrary states, with many more rollouts at the start st
          -ate, where the lower bound is read.                           
      198 -  It then rolls out π and the greedy π′ from the start state f
          -or the improvement bound. Each z-level                        
      199 -  margin is applied separately, and `lower = max(gap_lower, im
          -provement_lower)`. A certificate takes                        
      200 -  about 0.2 s on GridWorld and 0.8 s on 30×30.                
      201 -- `run_spmd(..., certifier=RolloutCertifier(), certify_every=k
          -)` adds `gap_lower`, `gap_upper` and                          
      202 -  `validation_observations` to the history. These run on their
          - own random stream and are not                                
      203 -  charged to the training budget, so training is unchanged.   
      204 -                                                              
      205 -**Do not use TOMC's training Q̂ for this.** Actions with π < π_
          -min are filled with 1/(1−γ), so a                             
      206 -deterministic, locked-in policy looks optimal. In Blackjack, "
          -draw below 7" has a true gap of 0.194,                        
      207 -TOMC's Q̂ gives bounds [0, 0], and the rollout certifier report
          -s a lower bound of 0.10. For the                              
      208 -optimal thresholds it reports 0.                              
      209 -                                                              
      210 -## Experiments and tuning protocol                            
      211 -                                                              
      212 -The protocol follows the handout. Each method grid-searches at
          - most three parameters, three values                          
      213 -each; `config.cfg` is validated so that more than three tuned 
          -parameters is an error. Every                                 
      214 -combination runs on 10% of the full observation budget with tu
          -ning seeds 100–102.                                           
      215 -                                                              
      216 -**Selection is model-free by default** (`[tuning] selection = 
          -gap_lower`). Every run's final policy                         
      217 -is certified (`certifier_*` settings), and the combination wit
          -h the smallest mean certified lower                           
      218 -bound on the optimality gap is chosen, with ties broken by the
          - mean upper bound. Only combinations                          
      219 -where every seed made at least one update are eligible. `selec
          -tion = exact_gap` (or                                         
      220 -`tune.py --selection exact_gap`) uses the exact gap instead, w
          -hich needs the model.                                         
      221 -                                                              
      222 -The chosen combination then runs on the full budget with 20 te
          -st seeds (200–219) and is compared with                       
      223 -the baseline by a two-sample z-test on the exact final gap (|z
          -| ≥ 2). `summary.csv` also reports the                        
      224 -mean certified lower bound. For nearly converged policies the 
          -lower bound is loose: on GridWorld the                        
      225 -chosen combinations certify 0.001–0.007 against exact gaps of 
          -about 0.075. It still ranked the                              
      226 -divergences in the same order as the exact gap.               
      227 -                                                              
      228 -| Method (`config.cfg`) | Estimator | Divergence | Tuned |    
      229 -|---|---|---|---|                                             
      230 -| `baseline` | TOMC | KL | step size × bias |                 
      231 -| `entropy_reg` | TOMC | KL, τ > 0 | τ × step size × bias |   
      232 -| `entropy_reg_annealed` | TOMC | KL, τ_k = τ₀/(k+1) | τ₀ × st
          -ep size × bias |                                              
      233 -| `true_online_td` | True Online TD | KL | step size × TD step
          - × λ |                                                        
      234 -| `bregman_euclidean` | TOMC | Euclidean | step size × bias | 
      235 -| `bregman_tsallis` | TOMC | Tsallis | step size × Tsallis ind
          -ex (0.2314, 0.5, 3.5) |                                       
      236 -                                                              
      237 -Budgets are 20M steps for GridWorld (one uniform-policy TOMC e
          -stimate takes 0.2–1.4M steps), 120k for                       
      238 -Blackjack and 3M for Inventory. `[method NAME @ PROBLEM]` sect
          -ions override settings for one                                
      239 -problem, such as TD's steps per iteration.                    
      240 -                                                              
      241 -## Testing, linting, type checking                            
      242 -                                                              
      243 -```bash                                                       
      244 -pytest                    # unit, statistical and end-to-end t
          -ests (perf and slow tests deselected)                         
      245 -pytest -m "perf or slow"  # throughput, the 10-minute pipeline
          - check, 30x30 SPMD                                            
      246 -ruff check . && ruff format --check .                         
      247 -mypy                      # strict, over src/, tests/, benchma
          -rks/ and tune.py                                              
      248 -```                                                           
      249 -                                                              
      250 -The suite covers every module:                                
      251 -                                                              
      252 -- **Environments:** the handout layout, 30×30 layouts, hand-co
          -mputed transitions, and simulator                             
      253 -  frequencies against the exact model.                        
      254 -- **TOMC and TD:** both are checked against straightforward re
          --implementations of the team's                                
      255 -  original algorithms (`tests/reference.py`), to 1e-10. TOMC i
          -s also tested for unbiasedness                                
      256 -  against the exact Q, and TD for convergence to it.          
      257 -- **Divergences and exact scoring:** the divergence contract; 
          -dense linear algebra and policy                               
      258 -  iteration for exact scoring.                                
      259 -- **SPMD and the tuning pipeline:** convergence with exact Q, 
          -and the full tuning pipeline end to end.                      
      260 -- **Numerical edge cases:**                                   
      261 -  - log 0, and KL with zero-probability actions               
      262 -  - η → ∞ and η·Q overflow                                    
      263 -  - Q ranges of ±1e308                                        
      264 -  - underflowing tail bounds                                  
      265 -  - standard errors from one seed                             
      266 -  - z-tests with zero variance                                
      267 -  - diverging TD step sizes, which stop the run with status `d
          -iverged`                                                      
      268 -                                                              
      269 -Warnings are errors in the suite, so a silent `divide by zero`
          - or `invalid value` fails a test.                             
      270 -                                                              
      271 -## Performance and memory                                     
      272 -                                                              
      273 -Measured on an Apple M3 (8 cores) with 7 workers; see `results
          -/benchmark.md`, regenerated by                                
      274 -`python benchmarks/bench.py --run`.                           
      275 -                                                              
      276 -- **Full pipeline:** the default `python tune.py` (GridWorld 1
          -0×10 and Blackjack, six methods,                              
      277 -  648 tuning runs, 240 full runs, each final policy certified)
          - takes **about 47 s** end to end. The limit is 10 minutes, and
          - the                                                          
      278 -  previous pure-Python version needed about 2 hours for a smal
          -ler experiment.                                               
      279 -- **Throughput:** TOMC samples about 30M steps/s on GridWorld 
          -and True Online TD about 10M steps/s,                         
      280 -  roughly 100× the pure-Python loops.                         
      281 -- **Memory:** peak memory is about 210 MB per process. The ori
          -ginal TOMC stored the whole stream                            
      282 -  (≈105 B per step, so 10 GB for 10⁸ steps). Returns now come 
          -from a ring buffer of the last ~350                           
      283 -  costs (γ^350 < 1e-16), so memory is O(|S||A|) for any stream
          - length. Exact models are sparse                              
      284 -  (0.3 MB instead of 26 MB for 30×30), TD features are index t
          -ables (29 kB instead of a 104 MB dense                        
      285 -  Φ), and TD history keeps V(s₀) rather than full Q snapshots.
      286 -                                                              
      287 -**GridWorld 30×30.** True Online TD + SPMD runs the full proto
          -col on 30×30 in about 3 minutes                               
      288 -(`python tune.py --problems GridWorld30 --methods true_online_
          -td`). **TOMC-based methods cannot run on                      
      289 -30×30 in any reasonable budget.** Under the uniform policy, 10
          -⁹ steps (31 s) still leave 461 of the                         
      290 -3600 required pairs unvisited, and coverage grows by only a fe
          -w hundred pairs per tenfold increase in                       
      291 -steps. Extrapolating that trend (measured up to 10⁹ steps), on
          -e estimate would not finish within the                        
      292 -~1.8×10¹⁰ steps a core samples in 10 minutes. With 90 hazards 
          -that send the agent back to the start                         
      293 -80% of the time, a random walk rarely reaches the far side, an
          -d no sampling speed changes that. The handout lets us adjust t
          -he environment if this happens, for                           
      294 -example with exploring starts or a lower reset probability; th
          -e handout's 10×10 grid has no such                            
      295 -problem.                                                      
      296 -                                                              
      297 -## Findings worth knowing                                     
      298 -                                                              
      299 -- **Blackjack's tuning budget is too small to separate methods
          -.** 10% of 120k steps allows about 2                          
      300 -  updates, so the grid search picks the largest step size, whi
          -ch jumps to the greedy policy at                              
      301 -  once. Every divergence has the same greedy limit, so all TOM
          -C-based methods end with identical                            
      302 -  Blackjack results. A larger Blackjack budget (or tuning frac
          -tion) is needed for a meaningful                              
      303 -  comparison there.                                           
      304 -                                                              
      305 -- **Entropy regularization vs. TOMC's fill value.** Once π(a|s
          -) drops below π_min, TOMC fills                               
      306 -  Q(s, a) with the pessimistic 1/(1−γ) = 10. The regularized s
          -tep then drives that action towards                           
      307 -  exp(−10/τ). τ keeps probabilities positive where plain SPMD 
          -underflows to exactly 0, but not far                          
      308 -  from 0.                                                     
      309 -- **Constant-step KL-PMD with exact Q** reaches a gap of about
          - 1e-5 quickly, then improves at O(1/k):                       
      310 -  in near-tie states the advantage is about 1e-8, so probabili
          -ty moves very slowly there.                                   
      311 -- **Results differ numerically from earlier runs.** Actions ar
          -e now drawn with one uniform number                           
      312 -  per step inside compiled code, so random streams differ from
          - the old `rng.choice` loops. The                              
      313 -  algorithms themselves are unchanged, and the tests check tha
          -t against the original code.                                  
       70 +1. **Bregman divergences** (`enhancements/bregman_spmd/`): `KL
          +` (the baseline step), `Euclidean`                            
       71 +   (exact projection onto the simplex) and `Tsallis(index)` (p
          +er-state Newton solve for the                                 
       72 +   normalizing multiplier). Config methods: `baseline`, `bregm
          +an_euclidean`, `bregman_tsallis`.                             
       73 +2. **True Online TD(λ)** (`enhancements/true_online_td/`): `Tr
          +ueOnlineTD(eta, lam, n_steps)`                                
       74 +   replaces TOMC as the Q estimator (van Seijen & Sutton, 2014
          +), with tabular or aggregated features.                       
       75 +   Config method: `true_online_td`.                           
       76 +3. **Entropy-regularized SPMD** (`enhancements/entropy_reg_spm
          +d/`): `tau > 0` adds τ·Σ π log π to the                       
       77 +   sampled costs and uses the closed-form KL step π ∝ π^{1/(1+
          +ητ)} exp(−ηQ/(1+ητ)); `AnnealedTau`                           
       78 +   gives τ_k = τ₀/(k+1). Config methods: `entropy_reg`, `entro
          +py_reg_annealed`.
